Advertisements
Portals
University of Baltistan
SKARDU
Department of Archaeology and Heritage Studies
Learning that Shapes Change. Preserving and promoting the rich cultural heritage of Gilgit-Baltistan.
Established: 2023
HEC Recognized
140+ Students
Introduction
The first comprehensive envisioned document titled “Future Plan of the University of Baltistan Skardu” is a comprehensive university strategic plan that was endorsed by the statutory bodies as well. The Future Plan has four bullet points related to Heritage Studies and Archaeology. The top one was: Institute of Archaeology, Cultural Studies, Buddhism, Bon tradition, and Islam with museum. Other envisioned points are: Establishment of ‘Kesar Cultural Center’ for Heritage Studies; Establishment of an International Center for Study and Preservation of Balti and other local languages; Establishment of an Area Study Center for Tibetan Studies (Tibet, Ladakh & Little Tibet).
Based on this Future Plan, the university continued its deliberations to implement these initiatives. Baltistan shares a deep cultural lineage with Tibet and Ladakh. The region’s archaeological rock carvings, inscriptions, architecture, and artifacts depict the great civilizations of the Bon tradition, Buddhism, Islamic Sufism, and also provide evidence of other religious traditions such as Jainism, Christianity, and Judaism. Together, these features make this part of the world an open museum of civilizations. The University of Baltistan, realizing its role as a key guardian and custodian of this heritage, has been engaged in deliberations to establish a formal institute or department.
As a result of the efforts of the worthy Vice Chancellor and the first Director of External Linkages, Dr. Zakir Hussain Zakir, Boston Architectural College (USA) was ready to submit a joint project on heritage conservation to the US Embassy for funding. The proposal was jointly developed, and after a lengthy process of evaluation, it was approved.
Following the project approval, the Office of the Director Academics placed the agenda for the establishment of the “Department of Archaeology and Heritage Studies” before the 5th Academic Council, and it was approved. For the establishment of this department, resources for a major part of human resource development, learning materials, curriculum development, library, digital archives, and an archaeological laboratory would be available from the project.
As per the agreement with the partners, the university developed an international standard curriculum for BS Archaeology and Heritage Studies in collaboration with experts from Boston Architectural College (USA). With this curriculum, which is aligned with the HEC guidelines, the BS program in Archaeology and Heritage Studies has been started. The process of developing the vision, mission, objectives of the department, program objectives, and learning outcomes has been finalized. The selection of courses, with a bifurcation into ‘Archaeology’ and ‘Heritage Studies’ tracks from the third year, has been agreed upon. For practical learning, an Archaeology Field School is being established in Shigar, and the development of Digital Archives is in progress.

Vision & Mission
Our Vision
To become a leading academic center dedicated to understanding, preserving, and critically engaging with the cultural heritage and social life of Gilgit-Baltistan through indigenous, decolonized, and practice-oriented scholarship
Our Mission
The Department of Archaeology and Heritage Studies aims to educate socially responsible graduates by integrating archaeology, heritage studies, and sociology to document the past, understand ongoing social change, and strengthen indigenous knowledge through research, fieldwork, and community engagement.
Core Values
Indigenous Knowledge
Academic Integrity
Decolonized Learning

Cultural Preservation

Portals
University of Baltistan
SKARDU
Department of Languages and Cultural Studies
Revitalizing Heritage, Amplifying Perspectives, Transforming Futures.
Established: 2012
HEC Recognized
404+ Students
Introduction
The Department of Languages and Cultural Studies was established in 2012 as part of Karakoram International University, Skardu Campus, which was later upgraded in 2017 to a federally chartered university, University of Baltistan, Skardu. Since its inception, the department has been dedicated to excellence in research and teaching in languages, literatures, and cultures. It is keen to provide a dynamic academic and research environment, aimed at equipping students with strong linguistic, literary, analytical, and intercultural competencies, to prepare them for a range of professions in academia, research, media, public service, and others. The outcome-based learning provided by the department fosters critical inquiry and the convergence of cutting-edge digital and interdisciplinary skills and approaches.
Apart from its usual academic focus, the department particularly focuses on documentation, preservation, and critical study of the rich literary, linguistic, and cultural heritage of Gilgit-Baltistan, rooting it in the broader national and international milieu. From its establishment, the department has always upheld a key role in shaping the academic identity of the institution. Today, it proudly stands as a leading department in intellectual vigor and rigor, research production, and meaningful contribution to the academic, literary, linguistic, and cultural landscape of Gilgit-Baltistan, Pakistan, and beyond.

Vision & Mission
Our Vision
The Department of Languages and Cultural Studies, University of Baltistan, Skardu, envisions becoming a leading center of excellence, in line with the overall vision of UoBS of ‘harnessing the unique natural heritage’ of Gilgit-Baltistan and Pakistan, by promoting linguistic, literary, and cultural scholarship for the preservation, promotion, and critical study of regional languages, literatures, and cultures alongside global traditions. The department envisions itself as a center that fosters intellectual inquiry, cultural research, documentation, and preservation, and focuses on impactful research and socio-cultural development, contributing to the academic advancement of Gilgit-Baltistan, Pakistan, and the wider world.
Our Mission
The mission of the Department of Languages and Cultural Studies at the University of Baltistan, Skardu, is to promote excellence in teaching, research, and community engagement in the fields of language, literature, culture, and intercultural studies. The department is committed to preserving and promoting the rich literary, linguistic, and cultural heritage of Baltistan and the broader Himalayan–Karakoram region, while fostering critical thinking, intercultural understanding, and global perspectives. Through innovative academic programs, scholarly research, and inclusive dialogue, the department aims to develop competent, ethical, and socially responsible graduates who make meaningful contributions to local, national, and international communities.
Core Values

Linguistic and Literary Scholarship

Portals
University of Baltistan
SKARDU
Department of Tourism and Hospitality Management
Developing Professionals for the Tourism Industry of Gilgit-Baltistan
Established: 2018
HEC Recognized
203+ Students
Introduction
The Department of Tourism and Hospitality Management at the University of Baltistan, Skardu, established in November 2018, aims to address the growing need for educated and well-trained tourism professionals in Gilgit-Baltistan. This region, representing Pakistan’s premier attraction for international and national tourists, is home to some of the world’s highest peaks, including the second-highest, K2. The vast glaciers, valleys, rivers, lakes, orchards, and rich cultural heritage of Gilgit-Baltistan offer huge potential to develop tourism as a robust industry. Our department is strategically positioned to create a tourism-friendly business environment in this region, located at the intersection of four great mountain ranges: the Himalayas, Karakoram, Hindukush, and Pamir. It lies along the historic Silk Route, now transformed into the China Pakistan Economic Corridor (CPEC), further enhancing its significance. We aim to develop a cadre of tourism professionals equipped with the necessary skills to manage and promote tourism effectively. Our curriculum combines theoretical knowledge with practical experience, preparing students for careers in tourism and hospitality management. We currently offer a 2-year Associate Degree and a 4-year BS in Tourism and Hospitality Management, with plans to expand our programs to include comprehensive undergraduate options like Culinary Arts Management, as well as postgraduate studies. Collaborations with local and international organizations, such as the Gilgit-Baltistan Tourism Department, PTDC, the Hotel Association, the Tour Operator Association, the Photography Association of Gilgit-Baltistan, UNWTO, and WTTC, will provide opportunities for hands-on training and job placements.

Vision & Mission
Our Vision
To be a leading center of excellence in tourism and hospitality education, promoting sustainable tourism development and cultural preservation in Gilgit-Baltistan, while empowering students to become global leaders in the industry.
Our Mission
The Department of Tourism and Hospitality Management at the University of Baltistan is dedicated to providing high-quality education and training that equips students with the skills and knowledge necessary to excel in the tourism and hospitality industry. Through strategic partnerships, hands-on training, and a curriculum that blends theory with practical experience, we aim to develop professionals who can effectively promote and manage tourism. Our commitment extends to enhancing the local economy, preserving natural and cultural heritage, and contributing to the Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs) through innovative and sustainable tourism practices.
Core Values
Sustainable Tourism Development
Cultural Heritage Preservation
Industry-Ready Professional Skills
Community Engagement and Development

University of Baltistan
SKARDU
Department of Educational Development
Developing Exemplary Educators for Transformative Learning
Established: 2012
NACTE Visited
513+ Students
Introduction
The Department of Educational Development is one of the pioneering academic units of the University of Baltistan, Skardu, Pakistan. It is dedicated to promoting excellence in teacher education, educational research, and professional development within the regional and national context. The department aims to produce competent teachers, teacher educators, educational managers, and leaders equipped with relevant knowledge, pedagogical skills, ethical values, and a strong foundation in research and scholarship.
The department currently offers BS Education, B.Ed. Education, and MPhil in Education programs in line with the guidelines of the Higher Education Commission (HEC) of Pakistan. These programs are designed to integrate theory with practice, fostering critical thinking, reflective practice, and innovation among students. Through its academic and research initiatives, the department contributes to improving the quality of education and addressing contemporary educational challenges at both regional and national levels.

Affiliation Process & Policies
Guidelines and procedures for colleges seeking affiliation with University of Baltistan, Skardu
Affiliation Process Steps
1
Application Submission
Submit application form with required documents at least 3 months prior to intended affiliation start date.
• Complete application form (Annexure A)
• Application processing fee
• Institutional registration documents
2
Inspection & Evaluation
University Inspection Committee visits the institution to assess facilities and infrastructure.
• Physical infrastructure inspection
• Academic facilities assessment
• Faculty qualifications verification
• Library and laboratory evaluation
3
Committee Review
Affiliation Committee reviews inspection report and makes recommendations.
• Inspection committee report analysis
• Committee deliberation and evaluation
• Recommendation to Senate/Syndicate
4
Approval & Agreement
Senate/Syndicate grants approval and formal agreement is signed.
• Senate/Syndicate approval
• Formal agreement signing
• First year provisional affiliation
• Fee payment and documentation
Minimum Requirements for Affiliation
Infrastructure Requirements
Minimum 0.5 acre (4 kanals) land with independent building
Classrooms (16' x 32') for each section (max 40 students)
Science labs (20' x 40') for experimental subjects
Adequate library with seating for 10% of students
IT lab with internet connectivity
Academic Requirements
50% permanent faculty with Master's degree
25% faculty with MS/M.Phil degrees
Student-teacher ratio 1:20 (Science), 1:30 (Others)
50 reference books & 200 supplementary books per subject
Subscription to 5 impact factor journals
Financial Requirements
Private colleges: Rs. 500,000 security deposit
Working capital: Rs. 2.0 million
Tangible assets: Minimum Rs. 5.0 million
Endowment fund: Rs. 1.0 million (private colleges)
Legal Requirements
Registration under Companies Ordinance/Societies Act
Corporate body ownership (not individual/family)
Provincial Government/HEC registration
Memorandum of Association and objectives
Affiliation Fee Structure
S.No
Fee Type
Amount (Rs.)
Remarks
1
Security Fee
500,000
From Private Colleges only
2
Application processing & affiliation visit fee per program
25,000 (Baltistan Region)
50,000 (Other Regions)
First time for each new program
3
Follow-up Fees per program
12,000 (Baltistan)
25,000 (Other Regions)
Based on number of programs
4
Student Registration Fee
1,500 per student
One time for entire program
5
Course Registration Fee
200 per course per semester
Every semester
6
Examination Fee
400 per course per semester
Every semester
Ready to Apply for Affiliation?
Download the complete application package including forms, guidelines, and checklist.
Contact Affiliation Office

Page 2 of 15 
 
 
 
ADMISSION POLICY 2021 
UNIVERSITY OF BALTISTAN, SKARDU 
University of Baltistan, Skardu (UoBS) shall make admissions to its degree programs, 
Diplomas, and Certificate Courses duly approved by the relevant bodies by adopting a 
procedure prescribed in the admission policy and the university calendar. A new program shall 
be initiated by the Head of the (relevant) Department and is duly approved by the Dean of the 
Faculty, the Univ ersity Admission Committee  (UAC), the Academic Council, and the 
Syndicate.  
At present, UoBS is offering admissions to the following programs in the teaching departments. 
A. Bachelor Studies (BS) 4 Years 
1. BS English Linguistics and Literature 
2. BS Education 
3. Bachelor of Business Administration 
4. BS Biological Sciences (Zoology/Botany) 
5. BS Chemistry 
6. BS Computer Sciences 
7. BS Mathematics 
8. BS Archaeology and Heritage Studies 
9. BS Environmental Sciences 
10. BS Tourism and Hospitality 
B. Associate Degree (AD) 2 Years 
1. Associate Degree in Tourism and Hospitality 
2. Associate Degree in Archaeology and Heritage Studies 
C. Diploma Courses 
1. One-year Diploma in Tourism and Hospitality  
2. One-year Diploma in Environmental Sciences  
3. Postgraduate Diploma in Environmental Sciences 
D. MS/MPhil  
1. MPhil Education  
2. MS Business Management  
3. MS Botany  
4. MS Zoology  
5. MS Chemistry  
6. MS Mathematics  




Page 3 of 15 
A copy of the printed Admission Policy UoBS  2021 shall be made available in all the  
departments and affiliated colleges as well as the university library and on the website for  the 
information and guidance of the students, parents, and the public at large.  
Admission Regulations in General 
The following shall be the rules and regulations of admissions for all the Degree Programs, 
Diplomas, and Certificate Courses.  
1. Admissions shall be made strictly on merit (including the reserved seats, however,  
their merit will be determined separately) in accordance with the criteria laid down by 
the University Admission Committee (UAC) from time to time. 
2. The UAC shall announce the Admissions Campaign each year in September/October 
and shall close the admission campaign by the following November before winter 
vacations. 
3. Merit shall be determined based on the required terminal degree. 
4. The 3rd division in the terminal degree shall not be acceptable for admission to UoBS 
in any Degree Program.  
5. The University Admission Committee (UAC) shall have the power to decide on issues 
and hardship cases according to the Admission Policy UoBS. However, the committee 
shall be bound to seek the anticipatory approval of the Vice-Chancellor and the 
Academic Council in all such cases. 
6. The UAC shall have the power to revise the admission criteria, before launching the 
admission campaign, however, with the anticipatory approval of the Vice-Chancellor 
and the Academic Council. 
7. For all the programs class size shall not be less than 10; however, for Ph.D. Programs 
shall not be less than five (05). However, under special circumstances, the minimum 
class size may be reduced to five (05) with special permission from the competent 
authority 
8. Any student, who had previously been rusticated, expelled, or whose entry in an y of 
the Departments/Colleges/Institutes/Centres was banned for any reason whatsoever at 
any time during his/her academic career, or any person declared persona non-grata 
shall not be admitted to any program or class (one session) without the permission of 
the Vice-Chancellor on the recommendations of the Chairperson,  followed by the 
Director Student Affairs (DSA) and the concerned Dean office. The Office of the DSA 
will circulate a complete list of such students to all the Departments,  against whom 
any kind of disciplinary action has been taken. The case of admission/re-admission of 
all such students will be processed in light of the university regulations.  
9. All difficulties connected with the admissions shall be resolved by the UAC in  
consultation with the Chairperson/Principal/Head of the Department; and shall deem  
to be settled with the approval of the Vice-Chancellor later.  
10. Any Reserved Seat which remains unfilled at the closing date (or extended period),  
shall not be transferred to the Merit Seats.  


Page 4 of 15 
11. Age limit  
i. A candidate for the BS Program shall not be older than 26 years on the last date 
fixed for the receipt of the applications for admission. However, in special cases, 
as decided by the University Admission Committee  (UAC), and if seats are  
available, the candidates above 26 years of age can be reconsidered for 
admission if he/she meets the merit criteria otherwise. 
ii. Age limit shall not be applicable to the following categories of candidates: 
 Employee/Spouse seeking admission against the reserved seats 
 Pakistani Armed Forces  
 Government Teacher seeking admission against the reserved seats 
iii. The age limit for government employees / autonomous bodies shall be followed 
as per Government rules if they submit the NOC and study leave from the  
competent authority to pursue their studies at UoBS. However, the seat shall not 
exceed their prescribed quota.  
iv. The age limit shall not be applicable to B.Ed., MBA Executive , and 
MS/MPhil/Ph.D. Programs.  
12. If two or more candidates fall at the same position on the list of merit, the older one  
shall be placed higher on the merit list.  
13. A candidate having a qualification/certificate/degree without English as a 
compulsory/elective/optional subject shall not be admitted to any program at UoBS. 
14. If equivalence of any qualification/certificate/degree is required, the case shall be  
forwarded with comments from the HoD of the Department to the Chairperson  
Admission Committee for final decision before the last merit list is displayed.  
15. The admission of applicants from other universities shall be subject to the production 
of the Equivalence Certificate  (if required)  from the university/Board before 
admissions are closed.  
16. A uniform policy, as laid down in the Admission Regulations, to determine the merit 
shall be observed in all the Departments/Colleges/Institutes/Centres/Sub-Campuses. 
17.  Criteria for admission of Foreign Studen ts shall be determined by the University  
Admission Committee (UAC). 
18. All the candidates shall be required to show their  original documents/ certificates; 
however, the office will not retain their original documents. And the candidates are 
required to submit the following documents to the Admissions Office of the UoBS for 
consideration of the merit list: 
 Admission Processing Fee Slip 
 Two copies of all the a cademic recor ds (Transcripts , Degrees, Equivalence 
Certificates, etc)  
 Copy of domicile certificate  
 Three passport-size photographs  
 Copy of computerized national identity card (CNIC) or form ‘B’  


Page 5 of 15 
 An affidavit signed by the  candidate and countersigned by their  
mother/father/guardian guaranteeing that the applicant shall not violate  the 
policy, statutes, rules, and regulations of the UoBS; moreover, they shall  
respect university discipline and shall abide by all the rules and regulations of 
the UoBS issued from time to time.  
19. After having deposited the due amount of fees at Bank, the students shall submit the  
receipt of their Fee Deposit Slip (FDS) in the Admission Office so that he/she may be 
issued their Roll Number of the Department. 
20. The dues other than those notified by the University shall not be charged by the  
Departments from the students/candidates.  
21. The candidate shall have to deposit their fee as notified by the UoBS within the due  
date. After hav ing passed the due date of fee submission, the candidate’s right of  
admission shall stand canceled. However, if there is no other eligible candidate in the 
waiting, and the seat is lying vacant, the candidate shall seek permission from the 
UAC and shall su bmit the due amount fee with a fine (prescribed by UAC and 
Treasurer Office) after the due date to secure his/her right of admission.  
22. Grade Point Average ( GPA) criteria of UoBS shall be applicable for converting 
Cumulative Grade Point Averages ( CGPAs) to percentages for those external 
candidates who submit their transcripts with CGPAs only and  remained unable to 
provide the percentage of marks from their previous university/institute.  
23. All admissions made in contravention of this policy and the rules and regulations of 
UoBS shall stand null and void.  
24. In case a student remains absent from the class for seven (07) days without having  
leave approved by the concerned teacher and the HoD, his/her name shall be removed 
from the rolls. However, the students shall be eligible for re -enrolment within three  
weeks-time from the date of his/her first absence, on the recommendation of the 
Chairperson/HoD, the office of the Dean concerned, and the approval of the Chairman 
University Admission Committee (UAC). All such applicants must submit the 
prescribed fee of the university before applying for re-enrolment.  
25. The candidates desirous of seeking admission to any program at UoBS must have had 
their result of pre -requisite degree/certificate officially notified by the respective  
body/authority of their university/Board on or before the closing date of the  
admissions.  
26. All issues/queries connected with the admissions shal l be resolved by the University 
Admission Committee (UAC).  
27. As per policy UoBS shall not be bound to provide hostel and transport facilities to the 
students.  
28. Only the candidates having Qualifications/Degrees/Certificates duly recognized by 
UoBS shall be eligible to apply for admission to UoBS.  
29. The UAC shall be authorized to increase or decrease the number of regular seats 
according to the student -teacher ratio as prescribed by HEC (and the accreditation 
councils) for BS programs, Diplomas, and Certificate Courses; and the availability of 


Page 6 of 15 
research supervision slots for MPhil/Ph.D. programs with the consent of relevant HoD 
and concern Dean. 
30. The Vice -Chancellor shall be authorized to increase or decrease the number of 
reserved seats for BS programs.  
31. A student/graduate/alumnus may be admitted/re-admitted to a second or subsequent-
Degree program at UoBS as per policy.  
32. The degree courses of B.Ed. and MBA Executive can be offered in parallel with other 
degrees in the same session or same year. Managing the clash in class timings of their 
classes shall solely be the reasonability of the student himself/herself.  
33. The migration of students from another HEC-recognized university to UoBS shall be 
governed by the Migration Policy of UoBS. The University Admission Committee 
(UAC) and an ad-hoc technical committee of the concerned Department, after having 
equated the courses and the credits earned previousl y by the candidate, shall decide 
on the admission offer to such candidates.  
34. The applicant shall be responsible to provide an Equivalence Certificate for O Level / 
A Level / other equivalent Intermediate Certificate or qualification from foreign 
institutes, issued by the Inter Board Committee of Chairmen (IBCC). Provisional 
Admission may be granted to such students subject to the provision of the Equivalence 
Certificate from the IBCC before the conduct of the First Examinations of the degree 
program for which admission was granted.  
35. The marks obtained by a candidate in the additional subject shall neither be counted 
nor given any weightage on the list of merit for admission.  
 
Inter-departmental/Inter-program Transfer of Admission 
36. In the event of vacant seats, inter -departmental/inter-program transfer of admission 
files and fees shall be permissible, within one week after the admissions closing date, 
on the recommendations of the Chairperson/HoD concerned, with the approval of the 
Dean of the Facul ty, provided that, all the merit criteria have been met. The 
Chairperson University Admission Committee shall finally confirm such admissions.  
Such transfers shall further be subject to: 
i. A student who succeeded to secure admission may, on a prescribed application 
form, request the Chairperson Admission Committee for transfer of his/her 
admission file/fee to a program where at least one seat is lying vacant. 
ii. A candidate who failed to secure admission may also, on a prescribed 
application form, request the Chairperson Admission Committee for transfer of 
his/her admission file/fee to a program where at least one seat is lying vacant. 
iii. Transfer request shall be initiated by the student/candidate who shall seek 
recommendations of the concerned Chairpersons/HoDs a nd the Dean of the 
Faculty, on the prescribed application form, which shall finally be confirmed by 
the Chairperson Admission Committee. 


Page 7 of 15 
iv. The transfer of admission file/fee shall only be valid after the approval of the 
Chairperson Admission Committee.  
 
Seat Allocation for BS Programs 
37. Seat allocation for BS programs is as follows: 
i. Every 4 -year Degree Program shall be enrolling 40 students in the order of 
merit. 
ii. B.Ed. and MBA Executive shall be enrolling 40 students in the order of merit. 
iii. 50% of the total seats in each program shall be reserved for the candidates 
having a domicile of Gilgit -Baltistan, and the remaining 50% will be filled in 
on open merit bases.  
iv. Foreign students shall be required to submit their applications through HEC, 
Government of Pakistan. There shall be 10 seats for foreign students in each 
program. Their merit will be defined separately. In addition to the normal fee, 
there shall be a Special Foreign Fee applicable to foreign students.  
v. One (01) seat shall be reserved in each BS Program (including Self -
Support/Afternoon/Evening programs, if any) in addition to the seats on merit 
as mentioned above, for the following categories  
A. Physically Handicapped Person 
B. Sports/Creative Talent 
C. Hafiz-e-Quran 
D. Pakistani Armed Forces 
E. Government Teachers 
F. Academic Regular Employee (serving/retired)/Spouse/Children 
G. Non-Academic Regular Employee (serving/retired)/Spouse/Children  
 
Admission to 5th Semester  
38. Candidates possessing a 2-year Associate Degree from UoBS shall be eligible to seek 
admission to their relevant BS Program at UoBS.  
39. Candidates possessing a 2 -year Associate Degree from any other HEC-recognized 
university shall be eligible to se ek admission to their relevant BS Program at UoBS 
only after having equated their courses and their credits earned previously with that of 
the UoBS in the relevant program. 
BS Exit Program 
40. The students of 4-year/5-year Bachelor programs (UOBS and Affiliated Colleges) shall 
be allowed to opt for an exit, a fter successful completion of the study of the first two 
years. Such students shall be awarded a 2-year Associate Degree.  


Page 8 of 15 
 
Eligibility Criteria for BS Programs 
41. Eligibility for BS or Equivalent Degree Programs:  
i. The candidates having HSSC/FA/FSc/Equivalent Qualification with at least 2nd 
division shall be eligible for admission to BS at UoBS.  
ii. The eligibility criterion for a specific program  will be decided by the relevant 
department according to the relevance of the subject studied previously and the 
HEC/Accreditation Council from time to time. 
iii. Entry Test for BS Programs shall be optional . (Decided by the concern ed 
department). 
Admissions Against Reserved Seats 
42. The admission of disabled persons shall be processed through the ad -hoc disability 
admission committee constituted to determine candidates’ (type and severity of) 
disability. The candidate shall be responsible to provide the disability certificate from 
the relevant authority. The department shall forward the cases of disabled persons to 
the ad -hoc disability admission committee. This ad -hoc committee shall submit the 
final list of selected candidates before the display of the third merit list. There shall be 
no relaxation in the given deadline.   
43. The admission on a sports/creative talent basis shall be processed through the ad-hoc 
sports admission committee constituted to conduct the trials for respective sports. The 
sports office shall be responsible to plan for the trials and submit a final list of the 
selected candidates before the display of the third merit list. There shall be no relaxation 
in the given deadline.  
44. Admission of the Hafiz-e-Quran candidates shall be processed through the ad-hoc Hifz 
admission committee of UoBS. The candidate shall be responsible to provide the Hifz-
e-Quran certificate from the relevant authority. The ad-hoc committee shall give an oral 
examination to the candidate(s) and shall send his/her recommendations to the 
University Admission Committee (UAC).  
45. The above ad-hoc committees will be notified by the chairman UAC.  
46. For the armed forces reserved se at, the candidate (children of armed force) shall be 
responsible to provide the nomination from the relevant office/quarter. Armed forces 
include all the government security organizations i.e., Pakistan Army, Police, Rangers, 
etc.  
47. Government Teachers seek ing admission on a reserved seat  shall provide a No 
Objection Certificate (NOC) issued by the Directorate of Education. 
48. Employees of UoBS seeking admission to any degree program are required to provide 
a No Objection Certificate (NOC) issued by the Registrar’s Office before the last date 
for submission of the admission application.  


Page 9 of 15 
49. Children/Spouse of UOBS employees seeking admission on  a reserved seat have to 
provide a nomination letter from Registrar’s Office.  
 
Admission Regulations for MS/MPhil/PhD  
50. Every MS/MPhil/Ph.D. Degree Program shall be enrolling the students in the order of 
merit according to the available slots for  supervision as per HEC guidelines; i. e. One 
PhD-approved supervisor (faculty member) can supervise a maximum of 12 research 
students of MS/MPhil/Ph.D. with the condition that the Ph.D. supervisee should be up 
to five (05), which may be extended to eight (08) in hard cases with the  prior 
permission.  
51. One (01) seat shall be reserved in each MS/MPhil Program (including Self -
Support/Afternoon/Evening programs, if any) in addition to the seats on merit, for the 
following categories:  
i. Physically Handicapped Person  
ii. Academic Regular Employee (serving/retired)/Spouse/Children  
iii. Non-Academic Regular Employee (serving/retired)/Spouse/Children  
52. The Chairperson/Head of the Departments shall, each year, communicate the number 
of available supervisory slots for MS/MPhil/Ph.D., prior to the university admission 
campaign. 
53. A candidate having relevant 16 years of education shall be considered eligible for  
admission to the corresponding MS/MPhil program.  
54. A candidate having relevant 18 years of education shall be considered eligible for  
admission to the corresponding Ph.D. program.  
55. Specific eligibility criteria for different programs may be provided by the  relevant 
Department / Institute / College / Centre.  
56. Entry Tests and interviews for admissions to MS/MPhil and Ph.D. programs shall be 
mandatory.  
 
Fee Transfer and Refund Policy 
57. Fee Transfer and Refund for Regular Programs: 
i. Fees and Funds (University and departmental dues) of a student shall be 
transferable to any other Department of UoBS subject to the approval of the 
Chairperson of the University Admission Committee. 
ii. All dues shall be refundable in case of cancellation of admission granted due to 
mistake, miscalculation, negligence, or omission on the part of UoBS. 
iii. Dues shall not be refundable in case of cancellation of admission if the applicant 
was found guilty of (i) providing incorrect information, (ii) 


Page 10 of 15 
concealing/distorting the facts, (iii) providing fake certificates , and (iv) using 
any other unfair means on the part of the applicant. 
iv. Any overpayment shall be refundable only after the fulfillment of all procedural 
formalities. 
v. The amount of security shall be refundable within three years after the 
completion of the degree of the student. 
58. Following tuition fee refund policy shall be applicable if a student withdraws his/her 
admission:  
i. Full (100%) Tuition Fee shall be refundable up to the 7 th day of convening of 
classes  
ii. Half (50%) Tuition Fee shall be refundable from the 8th – 15th day of convening 
of the classes  
iii. No Fee (0%) from the 16th day of convening of classes

Affidavit 
1. I solemnly declare that I am taking admission on a Regular / Full Payment / Foreign basis with my own will. I 
solemnly declare that I will pay all dues including Regular Fees / Full payment / Foreign charges, regularly for 
the entire period of my studies; and in the future, will not claim any type of Concession or Waiver of any type of 
fee/dues/charges payable towards me regarding my studies and will not submit any type of claim  in any court of 
law in any case.  
2. All the particulars mentioned in my application for admission and the documents submitted are true and correct 
and I fully understan d that if any of the statements made in the application form is found to be incorrect, my 
admission to the University of Baltistan, Skardu may not be made or if already admitted, would be liable to be 
expelled at any time during my studies. In that case, all fees and other dues paid by me shall be forfeited and any 
further departmental or legal action be taken which the university may deem fit to take.   
3. I also solemnly declare that, if admitted, I will abide by the discipline, rules, and regulations of t he university 
as enforced at present and made from time to time by the university authorities in the future. I will concern myself 
only with the academic activities and extracurricular activities, which are allowed by the university for the healthy 
growth, of the body and mind. I undertake that I will not take part in any political activity or agitation, and I will 
not involve in matters of discipline. The decision of the competent authority will be final and binding on me and 
it will not be challenged in any court of law.  
4. I will be regular in paying university dues and will be punctual in attending my classes. I will not remain absent 
from the university without prior permission from the university authorities.  
5. I undertake that so long as I am a student of the university, I will do nothing either inside or outside the university 
and its premises th at may interfere in its orderly administration and discipline or may bring the university or its 
administration into disrepute.  
6. I am taking admission at my own risk. If the related authorities (HEC and UoBS) cannot confirm my admission 
due to the reason that I do not fall in the category of admission and my academic qualifications do not fulfill the 
UoBS criteria of admission, I will abide by the decision and will not claim any right of admission at any stage in 
future.  
7. I fully understand that if I fail to clear the relevant subjects in fixed attempts or have a lower CGPA than the 
required for maintenance of admission in UoBS, I shall cease to be eligible for further Education in this institution. 
If I violate the above affidavit, I shall be liable for appropriate punishment(s) as prescribed in the admission 
regulations of the University of Baltistan, Skardu.

Awareness
Past Event
University of Baltistan Launches Three-Day Open Day 2026 to Promote Higher Education and Academic Excellence
Date
Aug 5, 2026
Venue
University of Baltistan Skardu
Event Overview
The University of Baltistan, Skardu (UOBS) officially inaugurated its Three-Day Open Day 2026, welcoming prospective students, parents, educators, and members of the community to experience the University's academic environment, research culture, and educational opportunities.
In his inaugural message, Vice Chancellor Prof. Dr. Masood Akhtar reaffirmed the University's commitment to providing quality higher education despite resource constraints.
"The University of Baltistan remains dedicated to making quality higher education accessible to students within its available resources and institutional capacity. Through this Open Day, we aim to demonstrate that university education is indispensable in today's knowledge-driven world while showcasing an academic environment that proudly reflects the rich cultural heritage and traditions of Baltistan," he stated.
The three-day event commenced with the participation of distinguished guests from diverse sectors of society, alongside a large number of students and visitors from educational institutions across the region. The University campus came alive with vibrant exhibitions organized by students from various academic departments, featuring innovative projects, research displays, handicrafts, entrepreneurial initiatives, and creative works that highlighted the talent and ingenuity of the University's academic community.
The first day also witnessed an enthusiastic turnout of prospective students, including delegations from Public School and College, Skardu, and Fatima Jinnah Women College, Skardu. Visitors toured the University's academic blocks, lecture halls, central library, modern laboratories, and other learning facilities, gaining first-hand insight into the University's teaching and research environment.
Students and faculty members from different departments delivered informative presentations introducing the University's undergraduate and postgraduate programs, research initiatives, academic achievements, and student support services. The sessions also provided visitors with valuable guidance regarding academic opportunities, career pathways, and the University's growing contribution to higher education and regional development.
Speaking on the occasion, visitors commended the University administration for organizing the Open Day, describing it as an excellent initiative to connect the University with the wider community. They noted that such events play a vital role in helping prospective students make informed educational choices while fostering awareness of the importance of higher education in the socio-economic development of Gilgit-Baltistan and Pakistan.
The University of Baltistan Open Day 2026 will continue over the next two days, offering visitors additional opportunities to explore academic departments, interact with faculty members and students, visit research laboratories, and learn about the University's diverse academic programs, innovation initiatives, and vibrant campus life.
The Open Day reflects the University's ongoing mission to strengthen access to higher education, encourage academic excellence, promote research and innovation, and build meaningful partnerships with schools, colleges, industry, and the wider community.

MS Chemistry
Advanced research-based program in chemistry with specializations in organic, inorganic, analytical, and physical chemistry.
Established: 2021
HEC Recognized
2.0 Year Program
30 Credit Hours
Program Overview
The MS Chemistry program offers advanced study in chemistry with specializations in organic, inorganic, analytical, and physical chemistry. Students engage in cutting-edge research under faculty supervision.
Program Highlights
Advanced Research
Focus on specialized areas of chemical sciences with faculty supervision.
Multiple Specializations
Choose from organic, inorganic, analytical, or physical chemistry.
State-of-the-Art Labs
Access to 6 specialized laboratories with modern equipment.
Research Publications
Opportunity to publish research in international journals.
International Collaborations
Research partnerships with national and international institutions.
Career Development
Preparation for PhD studies and industry careers.
Specializations
Organic Chemistry
Inorganic Chemistry
Analytical Chemistry
Physical Chemistry
Program Information
Program Code
MSCHEM
Duration
2.0 Years
Semesters
4
Credit Hours
30
Established
2021
Department:
Department of Chemistry
Curriculum Structure
Download Complete Curriculum
Career Paths
Research Scientist
Conduct research in academic or industrial labs
Quality Control Analyst
Work in pharmaceutical or chemical industries
University Lecturer
Teach at higher education institutions
PhD Candidate
Pursue doctoral studies
Environmental Chemist
Work on environmental analysis and remediation
Pharmaceutical Chemist
Develop new drugs and medicines
Research Areas
Environmental Chemistry
Research on water quality, pollution control, and environmental remediation techniques
Material Science
Development of new materials with applications in energy, catalysis, and electronics
Synthetic Chemistry
Development of novel synthetic methodologies
Medicinal Chemistry
Design and synthesis of bioactive compounds
Have Questions About This Program?

BS Chemistry
Comprehensive program covering organic, inorganic, physical, and analytical chemistry.
Established: 2018
HEC Recognized
4.0 Year Program
140 Credit Hours
Program Overview
The BS Chemistry program at the Department of Chemistry, University of Baltistan, Skardu, was established in 2013. The program offers comprehensive study of organic, inorganic, physical, and analytical chemistry with emphasis on both theoretical foundations and practical applications.
Program Highlights
Organic Chemistry
Study of carbon compounds and their reactions
Inorganic Chemistry
Study of inorganic compounds and coordination chemistry
Physical Chemistry
Study of physical principles in chemical systems
Analytical Chemistry
Chemical analysis and instrumentation
Specializations
Organic Chemistry
Inorganic Chemistry

Purchase of Printing Items
Stationery
Posted: 18 Jun 2026
Single Stage Two Envelope
Closed
Featured Tender
Invitation for E-bids for the Purchase of Printing Items for the University of Baltistan, Skardu, funded for FY 2026-27.
The University of
Baltistan, Skardu has reserved Funds for the procurement planned for FY
2026–27. The University intends to apply part of the proceeds of this
Fund to cover eligible payments under the contract for the “Purchase of
Printing Items.”
The University invites E-bids from eligible Bidders for procurement of goods described in the bidding documents on EPADS v2.0.
Single
Stage-Two Envelope will be used by adopting Least Cost Based Selection
(LCBS) Technique for the subject procurement, in line with the Public
Procurement Rules, 2004.
All
Bids must be accompanied by a Bid Security amounting described in Bid
Security Section in Bidding Document in the form of Call at Deposit.
Where Bid Security is not required, Bidders are required to furnish Bid
Security Declaration as specified.
E-Bidding
documents, containing detailed terms & conditions, specifications
and requirements etc. are available on e-Pak Acquisition and Disposal
System (EPADS) at:
https://vendors.epads.gov.pk/
Bidder(s)
are required to get themselves registered on EPADS v2.0 on or before:
Monday, July 6, 2026 – 11:00 AM. E-bids will be opened using EPADS v2.0
on the same day at: Monday, July 6, 2026 – 11:30 AM. Manual submission
of Bids shall not be entertained.
In
terms of Rule 48 of Public Procurement Rules, 2004, Grievance Redressal
Committee (GRC) is notified for the subject procurement and
notification copy is available on the procuring agency's website and on
Authority's website at (
www.ppra.org.pk
)

University of Baltistan
SKARDU
Department of Botany
Advancing Botanical Science for a Sustainable Future.
Established: 2018
HEC Recognized
147+ Students
Introduction
The Department of Botany, approved by the Academic Council in its 7th meeting held in 2023.The founding Head of Department, Dr. Alamdar Hussain, with a clear vision to harness the high botanical potential of the region and to promote excellence in botanical sciences. From its inception, the department has been committed to developing a strong academic and research culture aligned with regional priorities and global standards.
The department is well equipped with modern laboratory facilities and has successfully established a Herbarium and a dedicated Plant Pathology Laboratory. This laboratory has been established under EvK2CNR project(W4Development). It is supported by a team of well-qualified and dedicated faculty members who are actively engaged in teaching, mentoring, and innovative research. The department is involved in cutting-edge research and has initiated the MS program in Botany and aims to launch some other programs including short courses and certificates in the near future. Major areas of specialization include Ecology, Plant Physiology, Plant Pathology, Plant Taxonomy, Ethnobotany, Phytochemistry, Molecular Biology and Biodiversity Conservation.
In addition to academics and research, the department maintains strong collaborations with national and international organizations and contributes to society through effective linkages with the Agriculture and Forest Department Gilgit Baltistan, PARC, LUMS and other national universitites for sustainable utilization and processing of natural resources and active engagement with local communities. The department regularly organizes trainings, career counseling sessions, scientific conferences, and workshops, fostering capacity building, innovation, and professional development.
Department Statistics
147+

3

Vision & Mission
Our Vision
The Department of Botany envisions a world where the understanding, appreciation, and sustainable utilization of plant life are central to human well-being and environmental health. Through innovative research, education, and outreach, we aim to unravel the complexities of plant biology, promote biodiversity conservation, and contribute solutions to global challenges such as food security, climate change, and ecosystem restoration. Our department strives to cultivate a diverse and inclusive community of scholars, fostering creativity, collaboration, and leadership in botanical science for the betterment of society and the planet.
Our Mission
The mission of the Department of Botany is to conduct cutting-edge research in plant biology, from molecular mechanisms to ecosystem-level interactions, to deepen our understanding of plant life and its importance to the planet. We are committed to providing high-quality education and training to students, equipping them with the knowledge and skills to address current and future challenges in botany and related fields. Through active engagement with the community, we aim to promote awareness of plant diversity, conservation, and sustainable use, fostering a culture of environmental stewardship and scientific inquiry for the betterment of society and the natural world.
Core Values

Scientific Integrity
Innovation and Inquiry
Environmental Stewardship
Service to Society

Facilities & Laboratories
Botany Teaching and Plant Pathology Lab
The lab provides teaching lab facilities for undergraduate and graduate students as per HEC requirements. It enables students to study plant anatomy, genetics, taxonomy, physiology and a major area of specialization. Moreover, through the EvK2CNR, UNDP and the Italian agency for cooperation donated lab equipment for Plant Pathology, diseases, and developing plant protection strategies jointly with the Agriculture Department Baltistan Region.
Herbarium and Seed Lab
This lab provides basic facilities for the collection and preservation of plant germplasm and native seeds for study and research purposes. The lab also aligns with the national gene bank and herbarium.

University of Baltistan
SKARDU
Department of Zoology
Exploring Animal Life, Conserving Biodiversity, Shaping Futures
Established: 2016
HEC Recognized
206+ Students
Introduction
The Department of Biological Science
was announced by the then Vice Chancellor Dr. M. Asif Khan during the
visit of President of Pakistan his Excellency Mr. Mamnoon Hussain in
august 2016 on the high demand of public for higher education in Science
subjects. The Department was then approved in 4th academic council hold
on 26th October, 2016. The Department has established its teaching and
research laboratory at first floor of the existing building. The first
batch admissions were offered by the Department in spring 2018 in i.e.
BS Botany
BS Zoology
The
BS programs in Biological Science is to produce competent professionals
acquainted with the latest knowledge about the major disciplines of
Plants and Animal sciences and their challenges in this changing world.
This will enable the students to understand the principles of
organizations and inter-relationships in the biological systems with
particular reference to plant and animal diversity.
The foundation
courses in Botany includes, Biodiversity and Conservation, Plant
Anatomy, Genetics, Biochemistry, Biostatistics, and the major courses as
Physiology, Genetics, Ecology, Ethnobotany and Cell Biology.
The
Zoology as a subject is multidisciplinary in nature, involving study of
organisms and their genetic, morphological and physiological attributes,
their surrounding environment, and their role in conservation of
environment. The animal science is a combination of various disciplines
such as Genetics, Physiology, Ecology, Developmental Biology,
Microbiology, Parasitology, Entomology, Evolution, Taxonomy, Freshwater
Biology, Fisheries and Wildlife etc. This subject has significant role
in human resource development, food security, environmental
conservation, sustainable development and ultimately in alleviation of
poverty.
Recently MS Programs both in Botany and Zoology were offered in Fall 2021.
Department Statistics
206+
Total Students
4
Faculty Members
3
Academic Programs
1
Laboratories
6
Active Batches
Gender Distribution
27%
Male (56)
73%
Female (150)
Vision & Mission
Our Vision
The inception of the Department of Zoology, driven by a vision to facilitate advanced studies and research in Zoology. Our curriculum is strategically engineered to harmonize with global scientific trends and industry demands. By bridging the gap between academic theory and real-world application, we transform students into high-caliber professionals, ready to excel in the world’s most competitive industrial and academic sectors. Your journey toward global impact begins here.
Our Mission
The Department of Zoology is dedicated to fostering a dynamic academic environment that empowers individuals through intellectual curiosity and critical thinking. We achieve this by providing hands-on experience with cutting-edge computational tools, modern laboratory techniques, and advanced fieldwork methodologies. Nurturing creative, tolerant, and compassionate graduates who balance scientific expertise with a deep sense of social responsibility. Conducting high-impact research to address national challenges and contribute to the global body of zoological knowledge. Preparing highly skilled professionals capable of enriching public life and fueling innovation within academia, industry, and the economy of Pakistan.
Core Values
To promote Zoological sciences
Conservation of Animal Biodiversity
Develop career oriented applications

Facilities & Laboratories
Zoology Laboratory
The department has a Research Labs of containing Colorimeter, Spectrophotometer and latest microscopic photographic system. In addition to the instrument Labs, the department has a Zoology Museum which have huge collection of vertebrates and invertebrates. Since the establishment of the department, a considerable addition of insects has been made from various areas of Gilgit-Baltistan. The research in the filed of insect taxonomy and pathology (freshwater and marine), protozoans, helminths, insects and other arthropods is undertaken by trained and experienced staff faculty of Science and Zoology.

Wildlife and Fisheries

BS Computer Science
The BS Computer Science program provides a comprehensive foundation in computing principles, algorithms, data structures, and software development.
Established: 2013
NCEAC Accredited
4.0 Year Program
130 Credit Hours
Program Overview
The BS Computer Science program provides a comprehensive foundation in computing principles, algorithms, data structures, and software development. Following the NCEAC Curriculum 2026, this program prepares students for careers in software development, systems analysis, and technology leadership.
Program Highlights
Strong Foundation
Comprehensive coverage of computer science fundamentals including algorithms, data structures, and computer architecture.
Practical Focus
Hands-on experience through labs, projects, and internships with industry-standard tools and technologies.
Research Opportunities
Engage in cutting-edge research in areas like cloud computing, machine learning, and data science from early years.
Industry Alignment
Curriculum designed in consultation with industry experts to ensure relevance to current market needs.

BS Artificial Intelligence
The BS Artificial Intelligence program provides specialized education in AI fundamentals, machine learning, natural language processing, and computer vision.

Program Overview
The BS Artificial Intelligence program provides specialized education in AI fundamentals, machine learning, natural language processing, and computer vision.
Program Highlights
Machine Learning
Comprehensive coverage of ML algorithms, techniques, and applications.
Deep Learning
Advanced neural network architectures and their applications.
Computer Vision
Image processing, object detection, and visual recognition systems.
Natural Language Processing
Text analysis, language models, and conversational AI.

Seminar
11:00 AM
Past Event
University of Baltistan Hosts Webinar on Parasitic Zoonosis with Ev-K2-CNR & UNDP
Date
Jun 17, 2026
Venue
To be announced
Event Overview
The Department of Zoology, University of Baltistan Skardu, in collaboration with Ev-K2-CNR and UNDP, organized a one-day webinar on “Major Parasitic Zoonosis and Diagnostic Techniques of Domestic and Wild Animals of Gilgit-Baltistan” under the Water for Development (W4D) project funded by the Italian Agency for Development Cooperation (AICS).
The session was chaired by Prof. Dr. Masood Akhtar, Vice Chancellor, University of Baltistan. The Vice Chancellor appreciated Ev-K2-CNR’s support in establishing a dedicated zoonotic laboratory at the university through the provision of modern laboratory equipment, noting that the facility would strengthen practical research and scientific problem-solving in the region.
Concluding the two-day webinar series, which also included “Advanced Phytosanitary Training on Major Stone Fruits of Gilgit-Baltistan”, Prof. Dr. Akhtar acknowledged Ev-K2-CNR, UNDP, and AICS for their support and emphasized that such research-driven initiatives would advance animal and plant disease research in Gilgit-Baltistan.
International experts led the technical sessions. Prof. Dr. Luca Rossi provided training on the detection of endo- and ecto-zoonotic parasites, while Ms. Liliana addressed wildlife and livestock diseases, control measures, and the growing risk of zoonotic transmission.
Dr. Shahid Ali, Head of the Department of Zoology, highlighted the webinar’s role in fostering research collaboration and knowledge sharing on parasitic diseases affecting animals and humans in the region. Dr. Muhammad Ali noted that the project had strengthened diagnostic and research capacity by equipping students and researchers with advanced skills and techniques.
Project updates were presented by Mr. Riaz ul Hassan and Mr. Aurangzaib, representatives of Ev-K2-CNR, while Dr. Ishtiaq Hussain, Head of the Department of Botany and Focal Person of the project, stressed the importance of sustained capacity-building to enhance diagnostic capabilities in Zoology and Botany and to safeguard the ecosystems of Gilgit-Baltistan.

Observance
Featured
11:00 AM
Past Event
University of Baltistan Observes International Day to Combat Desertification and Drought
Date
June, 17, 2026
Venue
Main Campus
Event Overview
Skardu, June 17, 2026: The Department of Botany, in collaboration with the Department of Earth and Environmental Sciences, University of Baltistan (UoB), observed the International Day to Combat Desertification and Drought through a series of awareness and educational activities aimed at promoting sustainable land management and environmental conservation.
The event commenced with a welcome address by Dr. Ishtiaq Hussain, Head of the Department of Botany, who highlighted the significance of the day and emphasized the need for collective efforts to address the challenges of desertification and drought.
Prof. Dr. Masood Akhtar, Vice Chancellor, University of Baltistan, addressed the participants and underscored the importance of combating land degradation through awareness, research, and community engagement. He emphasized the role of academic institutions in developing sustainable solutions to environmental challenges keeping indigenous practices and SDGs and to support national economy through Agriculture and Livestock initiatives.
A symbolic awareness walk was organized from the Administration Block to Academic Block-II, involving faculty members, students, and university staff, demonstrating their commitment to environmental stewardship.
The technical session featured presentations by Experts of forest Department, Central Karakoram National Park (CKNP) and Deosai National Park (DNP), delivered a presentation on land degradation and forest conservation, on biodiversity conservation and sustainable natural resource management in protected areas.
Both Focal Persons Dr. Muhammad Qasim and Dr. Muhammad Akbar Zaigham, Department of Earth and Environmental Sciences and Botany shared their views in the concluding remarks appreciated the active participation of students and faculty and stressed the importance of interdisciplinary collaboration for environmental sustainability and acknowledged the contributions of the Vice Chancellor, guest speakers, faculty members, students, organizing team, and participants for making the event successful. The proceedings were efficiently conducted by Ms. Sajida Batool, (Master of Ceremony) of the program.
Faculty members, students, and staff from both departments actively participated in the event, reaffirming their commitment to environmental protection, sustainable land-use practices, and collective action against desertification and drought.
Event Details

Symposium
Featured
10:00 am to 03:30 pm
Past Event
National Symposium on the Importance of Yak Held at the University of Baltistan, Skardu
Date
Apr 13, 2026
Venue
University of Baltistan,Skardu
Event Overview
Yak is a Unique Treasure for the Region’s Economy, Say the Vice Chancellor and Other Experts Skardu (April 13, 2026): A one-day national symposium and workshop titled “Yak: A Unique Treasure of the Region” was jointly organized by the University of Baltistan, Skardu, and the Virtual University of Pakistan, Islamabad. A large number of national and international researchers participated in the workshop. The event was chaired by the Vice Chancellor of the University of Baltistan, Professor Dr. Masood Akhtar, who attended as the chief guest. In his address, he elaborated in detail—drawing on his expertise as a zoologist—on the importance, utility, and existing challenges related to yak research and development. This symposium, which has been organized consistently over the past four years, featured a visiting professor from Brazil who presented a paper on the anatomy of the yak. Other experts shared the latest scientific insights on yak genetics and diseases affecting the species. Dr. Ali Hamza participated online via video link and shared his experiences regarding entrepreneurship opportunities. The workshop was attended by the Deans and Directors of the University of Baltistan, along with MPhil and PhD scholars from the Department of Zoology. A significant number of teachers and students from Government Boys Degree College, Government Degree College for Women, and Public School and College were also present. Experts informed participants about contemporary research on yak in China and other neighboring countries and its impact on the local economy. In addition to the academic sessions, a one-hour essay writing competition was organized for students, in which approximately 22 students participated. Students from Government Degree College for Women secured first and second positions, while the third position was awarded to the University of Baltistan. The focal persons of the workshop, Associate Professor and Director Academics Dr. Ghulam Raza from the University of Baltistan and Dr. Tanveer from the Virtual University of Pakistan, spoke in detail about the organization, objectives, and significance of the program. The primary aim of the workshop was to raise awareness among the local community and farmers about improved yak breeding practices, modern management techniques, and the economic benefits associated with yak farming. In the second phase of the symposium, a “Farmer Field Day” will be held in Shigar, where a practical demonstration of yak will be arranged to benefit communities in areas where yak populations are limited. In conclusion, Vice Chancellor Professor Dr. Masood Akhtar appreciated the efforts of the entire team and termed the event a milestone for the sustainable development of the region.

Event
Featured
10:00 AM
Past Event
Dignified Pakistan Day Ceremony at the University of Baltistan Featuring Flag Hoisting and Patriotic Enthusiasm
Date
Mar 23, 2026
Venue
University of Baltistan,Skardu
Event Overview
A dignified and grand ceremony was held at the University of Baltistan, Skardu, in connection with Pakistan Day, March 23. Faculty members and students participated enthusiastically. The purpose of the event was to pay tribute to the martyrs of the Pakistan Movement and to highlight the importance of the Ideology of Pakistan.
The ceremony formally began with prayers for the prosperity and dignity of the motherland, followed by a flag-hoisting ceremony. The university premises echoed with slogans of “Pakistan Zindabad.” On this occasion, the national anthem was sung and special prayers were offered for the country’s security and stability.
Vice Chancellor Professor Dr. Masood Akhtar, in his message, encouraged the youth to strengthen Pakistan through education, innovation, and integrity. He stated that the youth are the true strength of the nation and the driving force that can lead the country forward through knowledge, creativity, and honesty. He expressed his commitment to building a prosperous and modern Pakistan where every citizen has the opportunity to progress.
University faculty members Dr. Zakir Hussain Zakir, Dr. Haji Karim Khan, and Nasir Abbas also addressed the gathering. The speakers expressed that March 23 is a historic day when the Muslims of the subcontinent determined their destiny and laid the foundation of the Two-Nation Theory. It was this very ideology that made the establishment of a free Islamic state on the world map possible.
The speakers warned that hostile forces are seeking to undermine Pakistan, the stronghold of Islam, but emphasized that the Pakistani nation stands united. Every citizen, they said, is like a solid wall, ready to make any sacrifice for the country’s stability. They stressed that to confront current challenges, the nation must revive the same spirit that prevailed at the time of Pakistan’s creation.
They pledged that together they would work for the honor and elevation of the green crescent flag. By following the principles set forth by Quaid-e-Azam Muhammad Ali Jinnah and striving to fulfill the dream of Allama Iqbal, the country would be steered toward progress and prosperity.
At the conclusion of the ceremony, sweets were distributed among the students, and a renewed commitment was made that through dedication and hard work in the field of education, Pakistan can be made a great nation.

Capacity Building Program
Featured
10:00 AM - 5:00 PM
Past Event
Three Days Capacity Building Training Program Concludes at University of Baltistan, Skardu VC Prof. Dr. Masood Akhtar emphasizes leadership, skills, and lifelong learning
Date
Jul 29, 2026
Venue
University of Baltistan,Skardu
Event Overview
The Capacity Building Training Program commenced with an inaugural session led by resource person Mr. Umar Saeed Qadri, focusing on institutional strengthening, leadership, professional development, and sustainable higher education, with Vice Chancellor Prof. Dr. Masood Akhtar emphasized that the training had been organized to conduct a comprehensive review of the administrative and academic systems of the university. The second day featured interactive sessions on people management in the contemporary era, professional etiquette, ethics, diversity, equity, and inclusion, equipping academic, administrative, and finance officials with practical leadership and management skills to enhance institutional performance and governance. The three-day Capacity Building Training Program for Principal Officers and Senior Management Team concluded at the University of Baltistan, Skardu, with a closing ceremony attended by university leadership, participants, and officials. On the final day, resource person Mr. Umar Saeed Kadri delivered sessions on Leadership in the VUCA Era & Industry 5.0, SDGs 2030: World vs. Pakistan, Progress & Roadmap, Modern Leadership and Management Practices from the Islamic Perspective, and an Integration Workshop that linked public financial management, ethics, leadership, and the Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs) to practical institutional scenarios. During the closing ceremony, Director Professional & Skill Development, Dr. Haji Karim Khan, welcomed the participants and presented a briefing on the training program. In his address, Vice Chancellor Prof. Dr. Masood Akhtar appreciated the valuable contributions of the resource person, Mr. Umar Saeed Kadri, and Dr. Haji Karim Khan for successfully organizing the training program. He emphasized that individuals should always remain prepared to face emerging challenges and changing circumstances. He noted that reading books broadens knowledge and enhances problem-solving abilities. He further stated that a true leader leads by example, believing in hard work and inspiring others through personal commitment. Highlighting the importance of practical competence, he remarked that "a degree is merely a piece of paper; it is the combination of education and professional skills that truly makes an individual successful." The Vice Chancellor further emphasized that such capacity-building initiatives are essential for strengthening the professional competencies of employees and enhancing institutional performance. He highlighted the importance of continuous professional development in higher education institutions, stating that regular training equips human resources with the knowledge and skills required to meet evolving workplace demands and global standards. He urged participants to embrace lifelong learning and continuously update themselves according to the needs of the time. Speaking on behalf of the participants, Dr. Zakir Hussain Zakir, Director Planning & Development (P&D), expressed gratitude to the university administration and the resource person for organizing a highly informative and practical training program that would contribute to improving institutional effectiveness. Certificates were distributed among the participants, while souvenir and gifts were presented to Mr. Umar Saeed Kadri in recognition of his outstanding services as the lead trainer. The ceremony concluded with a vote of thanks by Dr. Haji Karim Khan, while Mr. Abdul Rehman Mir, Lecturer, served as the master of ceremony.

11:30 AM
Past Event
University of Baltistan Organizes Awareness Rally for a Drug-Free Society
Date
Jun 18, 2026
Venue
To be announced
Event Overview
The University of Baltistan, Skardu organized a comprehensive awareness rally to promote awareness against drug abuse and encourage a drug-free campus environment. The rally was attended by a large number of students, faculty members, administrative officers, and university staff. Officials from the Anti-Narcotics Force (ANF), who specially visited from Gilgit, also participated in the event.
The rally was led by the Registrar, Dr. Mir Alam, and the Focal Person of the Drug-Free Campus Committee, Dr. Shafqat Hussain. Participants carried placards and banners displaying messages highlighting the harmful effects of drugs and emphasizing the need to protect the younger generation from this social menace.
Addressing the participants, Dr. Shafqat Hussain stated that maintaining a drug-free University of Baltistan is a shared responsibility of all stakeholders. He emphasized that a healthy, progressive, and prosperous society cannot be achieved unless this menace is eliminated from society. He further stated that youth are a valuable asset and raising awareness among them about the harmful effects of drugs is the need of the hour.
He added that the University of Baltistan will continue organizing awareness programs, seminars, and walks to eliminate drug abuse and promote awareness among students in order to create a safe and positive educational environment.
Registrar Dr. Mir Alam thanked the Anti-Narcotics Force officials for their special participation from Gilgit and appreciated their efforts in combating drug abuse. He stated that only through collaboration between educational institutions and law enforcement agencies can the younger generation be protected from social evils such as drug addiction.
At the conclusion of the rally, participants reaffirmed their commitment to actively contribute to the ongoing anti-drug campaign and fulfill their responsibilities toward establishing a drug-free society.
Furthermore, students of the Department of Chemistry played a key role in organizing and managing the awareness program. They actively contributed to planning the rally, guiding participants, preparing banners and placards, and supporting various administrative arrangements. The university administration appreciated the efforts of the Chemistry students and emphasized that active youth participation is essential to making this campaign against drugs successful.

10:00 AM
Past Event
Day of Kashmir Solidarity Celebrated at University of Baltistan, Skardu
Date
Feb 05, 2026
Venue
To be announced
Event Overview
The University of Baltistan, Skardu, celebrated
Kashmir Solidarity Day
with traditional enthusiasm and patriotic fervor. The day began with an inspiring
speech competition
held in the Senate Hall, where female students captivated the audience with their passionate addresses on the right to self-determination for the Kashmiri people.
Addressing the event, Vice Chancellor
Professor Dr. Masood Akhtar
stated that the Kashmir issue is not merely a regional conflict but a matter of humanity, whose solution lies in the aspirations of the Kashmiri people and the resolutions of the United Nations. He lauded the extraordinary resilience of the Kashmiri people and called upon the international community to end the ongoing violations of human rights.
Other speakers included
Dean of Social Sciences Professor Ashfaq Ahmad Shah
, who emphasized that the gathering was to show solidarity with the Kashmiri brothers and convey to the world that Pakistan stands with them.
Deputy Director Student Affairs Nasir Abbas
highlighted the purpose of the event, stating that the University of Baltistan continuously strives to promote national ideals. He added that Kashmir Day is observed to showcase the atrocities in Indian-occupied Kashmir and to affirm that Pakistan stands with the Kashmiri people in their struggle against oppression.
Associate Professor Botany Dr. Ishtiaq Hussain Mondoq,
the focal person
expressed gratitude and appreciated the organizers for their tireless efforts, while
Assistant Professor Dr. Muhammad Riaz
supervised the speech competition.
The results of the competition were as follows:
First Position:
Syeda Nosheen Zahra (Department of Educational Development)
Second Position:
Aliza (Department of Linguistics and Literature)
Third Position:
Ambreen Fatima (Department of Linguistics and Literature)
Special honors included
recitation
by Lecturer Muhammad Kamal and the
Naat of the Prophet ﷺ
presented by Director IT, Imtiaz Ahmed. The event was expertly moderated by student Razia Hameed. The judges for the competition were Dr. Muhammad Hassan, Dr. Muhammad Nazir, and Dr. Zulfiqar Ali.
In addition to the speeches, a
short rally
was organized from the main gate to the Admin Block. A large number of faculty, administrative officers, and students participated, holding banners and pamphlets condemning Indian atrocities in occupied Kashmir. The demonstrators stated that India’s oppressive actions have now been exposed to the world, and the international community recognizes India’s suppression of Kashmiri freedom. They affirmed that the Kashmiri people cannot be kept under force for long, as every child in the region is now awakened to fight for their freedom.
It is worth noting that the administration, faculty, and students of the University of Baltistan consistently observe national events with zeal and reaffirm their unwavering commitment to recognizing the
inalienable right to self-determination of the Kashmiri people
.

Assistant Sub-Inspector (ASI), Police Department
Kacho Zahid Hussain
BS Botany, University of Baltistan
Botany
Public Service
Law Enforcement
Success Story
Mr. Kacho Zahid Hussain, an alumnus of the Department of Botany, University of Baltistan, Skardu, has successfully joined the Police Department of Gilgit-Baltistan as an Assistant Sub-Inspector (ASI), showcasing how academic discipline, dedication, and perseverance can lead to distinguished public service.
Mr. Kacho Zahid Hussain completed his bachelor's degree in Botany from the University of Baltistan, Skardu, where he developed a strong foundation in scientific thinking, discipline, and problem-solving skills. During his academic journey, he remained committed to personal growth, academic excellence, and character building – qualities that later proved instrumental in his professional success.
After graduation, Mr. Zahid pursued his goal of serving society through law enforcement. His determination, consistent effort, and strong educational background enabled him to qualify for a competitive selection process, leading to his appointment as an Assistant Sub-Inspector (ASI) in the Police Department of Gilgit-Baltistan.
Today, he serves with integrity, responsibility, and dedication, contributing to public safety and community well-being. His journey stands as a source of inspiration for current students, demonstrating that graduates of the Botany Department can excel not only in scientific fields but also in diverse professional domains, including civil and public services.
About This Achievement
Kacho Zahid Hussain achieved
assistant sub-inspector (asi), police department while studying
BS Botany, University of Baltistan.
This remarkable accomplishment demonstrates the quality of education and opportunities available at University of Baltistan.
